What was found

1

Finding 1: deadlock (DEADLOCK_INDICATORS)

confirmed root cause
  • Certainty: confirmed root cause; severity: critical.
  • Found by: Long-blocked threads (RECIPE_LONG_RUNNING_BLOCKED_THREADS), Suspicious lock ownership (RECIPE_SUSPICIOUS_LOCK_OWNERSHIP), Deadlock (wait-for cycle) (RECIPE_POTENTIAL_DEADLOCK_PATTERNS). Evidence records: 6.

How the threads got stuck

Step
  1. deadlock_circul (3734211) takes lock g_mutex_a reconstructedSeen as the current owner of this lock. Where it took the lock was not observed.
  2. deadlock_circul (3734212) takes lock g_mutex_b reconstructedSeen as the current owner of this lock. Where it took the lock was not observed.
  3. deadlock_circul (3734211) asks for lock g_mutex_b, which deadlock_circul (3734212) holds, and blocks observedStopped at proc_thread_circular_deadlock.c:28: pthread_mutex_lock(&g_mutex_b);
  4. deadlock_circul (3734212) asks for lock g_mutex_a, which deadlock_circul (3734211) holds, and blocks observedStopped at proc_thread_circular_deadlock.c:38: pthread_mutex_lock(&g_mutex_a);
  5. The circle is closed: every thread waits for a lock the next one holds, so none can continue. observed5 samples over 0.70 s: no CPU time, same wait in every sample. The same cycle was seen in 2 separate observations.

Each thread must have taken its first lock before asking for the second. Which thread went first is not known; the diagram shows one possible order.

observed observed = read directly from the running processreconstructed reconstructed = follows logically from what was observed; the exact moment was not seen

The same, as text

Circular wait among 2 thread(s) and 2 lock(s):

  • worker_1 (tid 3734211) waits for lock g_mutex_b, which is held by worker_2 (tid 3734212).
  • worker_2 (tid 3734212) waits for lock g_mutex_a, which is held by worker_1 (tid 3734211).
  • worker_1 (tid 3734211) is blocked at proc_thread_circular_deadlock.c:28 in pthread_mutex_lock(&g_mutex_b).
  • worker_2 (tid 3734212) is blocked at proc_thread_circular_deadlock.c:38 in pthread_mutex_lock(&g_mutex_a).

The operating system gives all these threads the same name (deadlock_circul), so they are told apart by the function they run and their thread id.

Wait-for cycle

thread --waits--> lock --held by--> thread
worker_1 (tid 3734211) --waits--> g_mutex_b --held by--> worker_2 (tid 3734212)
worker_2 (tid 3734212) --waits--> g_mutex_a --held by--> worker_1 (tid 3734211)

Why this conclusion

  1. The thread states were sampled 5 times over 0.7023 s; the threads in the cycle stayed blocked in the kernel the whole time without using CPU.
  2. The same cycle was seen in 2 separate observations, so it is not a momentary overlap.
  3. Each thread in the cycle waits for a lock held by the next one, so none of them can ever continue: this is a deadlock.
Evidence
  • Blocked threads: 2; Thread 'deadlock_circul' (tid 3734210) stayed in ppoll (ppoll) for the whole 0.70s window with no CPU time and no context switch: long wait observed, cannot determine whether expected.; Thread 'deadlock_circul' (tid 3734211) stayed in futex (mutex g_mutex_b) for the whole 0.70s window with no CPU time and no context switch: long wait observed, cannot determine whether expected.; Thread 'deadlock_circul' (tid 3734212) stayed in futex (mutex g_mutex_a) for the whole 0.70s window with no CPU time and no context switch: long wait observed, cannot determine whether expected.
  • Suspicious/circular mutex ownership detected
  • Cycle verified persistent across threads

What to do next

  1. The two locks g_mutex_b and g_mutex_a are taken in opposite order: worker_1 (tid 3734211) (blocked at proc_thread_circular_deadlock.c:28) and worker_2 (tid 3734212) (blocked at proc_thread_circular_deadlock.c:38) each hold one and wait for the other. Open both places and make the order of the two lock calls consistent, for example always take g_mutex_b before g_mutex_a in both, or protect both with a single lock.
  2. To see each thread's full call stack, ask for a stack capture; that pauses the process briefly and needs your approval.

  • wall_clock_ms: 13378.512

Reference

Glossary

  • deadlock: threads wait for each other's locks in a circle, so none can continue.
  • lock (mutex): a flag a thread takes so that only one thread uses shared data at a time.
  • wait-for cycle: the chain thread -> lock -> owning thread -> lock ... that closes on itself.
  • confirmed / probable / could not decide: confirmed = proven by the evidence, probable = likely but not fully proven, could not decide = the checks gave no answer (this does not mean the process is fine).
  • zero-stop: the process was read while it kept running; it was never paused.
  • debug info (debug image): the build of the program that still carries variable and line information (DWARF).
